PREFACE
The present book ia n collection of articles which were published 
in the /rtdi'tm Historical Quarterly and the Calcutta Oriental Journal between 
1030 and 1M34, They contain the results of my cismiostiou of manu- 
scripts of the l^epal Darbar Library collection in 102$). J have thought 
it fit to publish them together in the form of a book, as my future 
studies in the Tantras will follow the lines suggested therein. Ah many of 
the manuscripts referred to in the article^ have little chance of being edited 
and published for a long time to come, I have added in an appendix detailed 
notices on them.
CALCUTTA: \
Sejufew&er, J939. j
P, C. bagchi.

ON THE SADHr\NAM\UA
The present volume which forms the Tome X JjI of the Gaekwad's 
Oriental Series, contains the remaining S&ti/tano*, 142 ' m number (Nos. 
171-312}. Hr. Uhaltaelisiryya is to he congratulated on the successful 
completion of his edition of the SatlAanamala, Most of thesa Sadfiauas 
are tying scattered in Tibetan translation in the /Jdlau Agyur and the worth 
of the edition would have been Certainly enhanced if they had been 
compared for settling the text. Blit still aur indebtedness to the editor 
is not minimiued in the least, for he is doing the work of a pioneer in this 
Held. The greatest importance of these texts is iconographies! and 
ritualistic. Dr. Bliattacharyya in his Indian BnddAitt Iconography has done 
justice to the i con og rap Ideal aspect of the work, but the ritualistic abject 
still remain? to be studied.
Id a learned introduction the editor has discussed some of the most 
important problems of Buddhist Tantras bearing on the present test. 
This introduction deals with : ^]) Magic in ancient India in which he finds 
the origin of the Tanlras j (2) Tantras in which he gives a general 
description of the Hindu and Buddhist Tantras j (3) Origin ami development 
of Vajrayana; (4) Chronology of the Vujrayfina t (&} Leading tenets of

SOME ASPECTS OP HUDDHIST MYSTICISM 
IN THE CA ll YAP A DAS
The Caryflpadas1 were published for the first limo by the late Dr. 
H. P Sh&strl from an oM manuaoripti discovered in Nepal* The work 
ls a collection of 50 Caryapadas, of which three are now missing ; they 
WiS'f) oom poll'd by various anthers like Sarahapiida r K&llhlipttdu, {aUff T 
Kreoacitrya), Bhusukn, Mom hi. all of whom appear in the list of b l Siddhan
